     11	
     12	# Phase 03 — Upload e Processamento de Vídeos
     13	
     14	## Objective
     15	
     16	Entregar o backend de upload e processamento de vídeos do StreamTube: serviço de armazenamento de arquivos (vídeos e thumbnails) em object storage S3-compatível (MinIO local), serviço de processamento em segundo plano (filas BullMQ + `video-worker`), upload resumível de até 10GB direto para o storage sem impacto na performance da API, pré-cadastro automático do vídeo como rascunho ao iniciar o upload, processamento automático após o upload (extração de duração e metadados com ffprobe) com geração automática de thumbnail a partir de um frame, URL única por vídeo (slug sem conflito) e reprodução via streaming e download do vídeo pelo usuário (dono) via URLs pré-assinadas.
     17	
     18	---
     19	
     20	## Step Implementations
     21	
     22	### SI-03.1 — Infra: dependências, FFmpeg na imagem e serviços Compose (minio, minio-init, redis)
     23	
     24	**Description:** Instalar as bibliotecas decididas com pins exatos, colocar FFmpeg na imagem de dev compartilhada e subir object storage (MinIO + bootstrap do bucket) e Redis junto com o backend — a infraestrutura que todos os SIs seguintes exercitam de verdade nos testes.
     25	
     26	**Technical actions:**
     27	
     28	1. Instalar dentro do container, com `npm install --save-exact`: `@nestjs/bullmq@12.0.0`, `bullmq@6.3.15`, `ioredis@6.0.0` (peer opcional obrigatório no BullMQ 6) (per `phase-03-videos/TD-01`), `@aws-sdk/client-s3@3.1152.0` e `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner@3.1152.0` (sempre na mesma versão) (per `phase-03-videos/TD-03`).
     29	2. Atualizar `nestjs-project/D … (+3000 chars)

  ⎿  914	### Authorization Matrix
     915	
     916	"Owner" = usuário autenticado cujo canal (`channels.user_id = sub`) é o `channel_id` do vídeo (per `phase-03-videos/TD-10`, Note AMB-2). Em Phase 03 todo vídeo é um rascunho não publicado, então não há acesso anônimo nem regras de visibilidade (per `phase-03-videos/TD-15`, Note AMB-4 — adiado para Fase 04/05).
     917	
     918	| Endpoint | Anonymous | Authenticated (não dono) | Owner | Restrição de status |
     919	|----------|-----------|--------------------------|-------|---------------------|
     920	| POST /videos | ✗ 401 | ✓ (cria o vídeo no próprio canal e passa a ser o dono) | — | — |
     921	| GET /videos/:id | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | qualquer status |
     922	| GET /videos/:id/upload | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `draft` (senão 409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS) |
     923	| POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `draft` (senão 409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS) |
     924	| POST /videos/:id/upload/complete | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `draft`; `processing`/`ready` = replay idempotente 200; `failed` = 409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS |
     925	| GET /videos/:id/stream | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `ready` (senão 409 VIDEO_NOT_READY) |
     926	| GET /videos/:id/download | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `ready` (senão 409 VIDEO_NOT_READY) |
     927	| GET /videos/:id/thumbnail | ✗ 401 | ✗ 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND | ✓ | `ready` (senão 409 VIDEO_NOT_READY) |
     928	
     929	A checagem de dono acontece **antes** da checagem de status: um não dono recebe sempre `404`, nunca `409`, para não vazar a existência nem o estado do vídeo. As URLs pré-assinadas devolvidas são credenciais portáveis até expirarem (TTL configurável); por isso os redirects levam `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
     930	
     931	### Error Catalog
     932	
     933	**Error response format:** herdado de Phase 02 (`phase-02-auth/TD-07`) — `{ statusCode: number, error: string, message: string }`. Novas classes ficam em `src/videos/exceptions/video.exceptions.ts`, estendendo `DomainEx … (+2312 chars)

     # CLAUDE.md
     
     ## Environment Startup Verification
     
     **Default behavior:** starting the environment means starting **only infrastructure services** (database, mail, etc.) — **never** start the NestJS application server unless the user explicitly asks to run/serve the project (e.g., "rode o projeto", "suba o servidor", "run the app").
     
     After starting infrastructure, always confirm the containers are up before proceeding:
     
     ```bash
     docker compose ps   # all services must show status "running"
     ```
     
     Then verify each infrastructure service is actually ready to accept connections — not just running:
     
     - **PostgreSQL:** `docker compose exec db pg_isready -U streamtube` — expect `accepting connections`
     
     Only start the NestJS dev server (`npm run start:dev`) when the user **explicitly** asks to run the application — never as part of "start the environment".
     
     ## Development Environment
     
     This project runs inside Docker. Always use the container for development:
     
     ```bash
     # Start containers
     docker compose up -d
     
     # Install dependencies (first time only)
     docker compose exec nestjs-api npm install
     
     # Run the dev server (watch mode)
     docker compose exec nestjs-api npm run start:dev
     ```
     
     Services:
     - `nestjs-api` — NestJS API, port `300 … (+1730 chars)

● Read(/Users/ricardorauberpereira/Downloads/MBA-Desafios/Upload_Processamento_Videos-StreamTube/docs/phases/phase-03-videos/library-refs.md)
  ⎿  120	## ioredis
     121	
     122	**Source:** `/redis/ioredis/v6.0.0` (Context7), the required BullMQ 6 peer for `phase-03-videos/TD-01`. **Pin:** `6.0.0`.
     123	
     124	- **Breaking changes in v6:** it requires Node ≥ 20, which `node:25.6.0-slim` satisfies, and **defaults to RESP3** (`protocol: 3`). Set `protocol: 2` to keep v5 wire behavior if a Redis-side incompatibility appears. Redis 8.x supports RESP3.
     125	- TypeScript import: `import { Redis } from 'ioredis'`. The default import still works but is slated for deprecation.
     126	- Useful options: `host`, `port`, `password`, `connectTimeout` (default 10000 ms), `lazyConnect`, `enableOfflineQueue`, and `maxRetriesPerRequest` (default 20; must be `null` for BullMQ workers).
     127	- Usually no direct ioredis code is needed: pass `{ host, port }` through `BullModule.forRootAsync`, and BullMQ creates the clients.
     128	
     129	**Compose image (TD-01 Note):** `redis:8.10.2-alpine`, started with `--maxmemory-policy noeviction`, plus a `redis-cli ping` healthcheck so that `depends_on: condition: service_healthy` works.

"overrides": { "typeorm": { "ioredis": "6.0.0" } }

Now only one copy of `ioredis` (6.0.0) is installed. If you'd rather not keep the override, the other options are dropping to `ioredis@5.x`, which drops the plan's 6.0.0 pin, or `--legacy-peer-deps`, which every future install would then need. Details are in `progress.md`.

## 0. Purpose

This guide helps you decide **what to test**, at **which layer**, and **how to set up tests** for each type of artifact in `nestjs-project`. When working on a specific artifact type, read the corresponding guide in `artifacts/` for the complete recipe. Supporting references (mock strategies, file conventions, gotchas) are in `references/`.

Artifact types covered: entities, services, controllers, modules, DTOs, guards, strategies, pipes, interceptors, filters, middleware. For anticipated types not yet in the project, see `artifacts/future-types.md`.

## 1. Testability Foundations

NestJS's DI container makes testing natural — `Test.createTestingModule()` lets you replace any provider with a mock via `useValue`, `useFactory`, or `useClass`. This is why unit tests mock at service boundaries rather than patching internal methods.

**Why module compilation tests matter:** TypeScript catches type errors at compile time, but NestJS resolves dependencies at *runtime* via the DI container. A missing `imports` entry, a wrong provider token, or a forgotten `exports` array causes a runtime crash that TypeScript cannot catch. Module compilation tests (`Test.createTestingModule({ imports: [MyModule] }).compile()`) are the only way to catch DI wiring errors before production.

**Mock boundary principle in NestJS terms:** Mock across module boundaries, not within. If `AuthService` depends on `UsersService`, mock `UsersService` in `AuthService`'s unit test — `UsersService` has its own tests. But use *real* `JwtModule` with test config because it's a configured library — mocking `JwtService.sign()` returning `'fake-token'` never catches a wrong secret, bad expiration, or malformed payload.

**Why integration tests aren't redundant with E2E:** E2E tests prove the HTTP contract (status codes, validation wiring, response shape). Integration tests prove the *database contract* (correct queries, constraint enforcement, transaction behavior). A service that builds a wrong `WHERE` clause will pass E2E tests with mocked repositories but fail in production. Both layers are required; neither substitutes the other.

**Configured dependency contracts:** When you configure a framework module (`JwtModule.register()`, `ThrottlerModule.forRoot()`, `CacheModule.register()`), that configuration is a contract. The library's tests verify its internals — they cannot verify YOUR config values. Test configured libs with real instances and test-appropriate config.

**NestJS 11 + Jest 30 + ts-jest 29:** The project uses NestJS 11 with Jest 30 and ts-jest 29.2.5. Jest 30 introduces ESM-first defaults — ensure `ts-jest` transform is configured in both unit and E2E jest configs. The `@nestjs/testing` module version 11 provides `Test.createTestingModule()` with full DI support. Note that `overrideProvider()` in the test module builder is the idiomatic way to replace providers without modifying module imports.

## 2. Testing Criteria

### Worth testing

- Services with branching logic (e.g., conditional flows for registration, login, password reset)
- Entity constraints and defaults — unique indexes, `select: false` fields, `@CreateDateColumn` behavior, cascade rules
- Service-to-database contracts — repository queries, TypeORM relation loading, transaction boundaries
- Service-to-external-system contracts — local storage uploads, SMTP sends via Mailpit, queue publishing
- Module DI wiring — every module with configured imports (`TypeOrmModule.forFeature()`, `JwtModule.register()`, `BullModule.registerQueue()`)
- Guard authorization logic — role checks, ownership verification, token validation flows
- Exception filter error mapping — domain exceptions to HTTP responses
- Controller HTTP contracts — status codes, validation rejection, auth enforcement, response shape
- DTO validation wiring — one E2E test per endpoint proving `ValidationPipe` is active
- Security boundaries — authentication flows (JWT), rate limiting, input sanitization
- Race conditions — concurrent video uploads, duplicate likes/subscriptions, optimistic locking

### NOT worth testing

- Controllers in isolation (unit tests) — they are thin delegation; test via E2E only
- Validation decorator behavior (e.g., does `@IsEmail()` reject invalid emails?) — trust `class-validator`; one wiring test per endpoint is enough
- Framework routing — trust NestJS `@Get()`, `@Post()` decorators work
- TypeORM persistence mechanics — trust that `repository.save()` persists; test YOUR queries and constraints
- Mirror tests — assertions that copy the implementation's return value
- Static entity field existence — if a column exists in the entity, TypeORM will map it
- Single-path service methods with no branching and no system boundary (e.g., `getHello()`)
- Guard/pipe/filter behavior that is pure delegation to a framework feature (e.g., `AuthGuard('jwt')` with no custom logic)

## 3. Feature Implementation Checklist

When implementing a new feature, use this checklist to ensure all artifacts have appropriate test coverage:

| Artifact created | Required tests | Guide |
|---|---|---|
| Entity (`*.entity.ts`) | Integration: constraints, defaults, `select: false` | `artifacts/entities.md` |
| Service with branching + DB | Unit: branch logic (mock repo) + Integration: DB contract | `artifacts/services.md` |
| Service with DB only (no branching) | Integration: DB contract | `artifacts/services.md` |
| Service with configured lib (JWT, cache) | Unit: real lib with test config | `artifacts/services.md` |
| Service with side-effect dep (email, storage) | Integration: real capture service (Mailpit) or local adapter | `artifacts/services.md` |
| Module with configured imports | Unit: compilation test | `artifacts/modules.md` |
| Controller | E2E only — do NOT write unit tests | `artifacts/controllers.md` |
| DTO | E2E: one validation wiring test per endpoint | `artifacts/dtos.md` |
| Guard (delegates to service for business logic) | E2E + Unit if complex internal logic | `artifacts/guards.md` |
| Guard (simple, delegates to Passport) | E2E only | `artifacts/guards.md` |
| Strategy (Passport) | E2E via guard | `artifacts/strategies.md` |
| Pipe (custom transformation/validation) | Unit | `artifacts/pipes.md` |
| Interceptor (response transform, logging) | Unit and/or E2E | `artifacts/interceptors.md` |
| Exception Filter | Unit + E2E | `artifacts/filters.md` |
| Middleware | E2E | `artifacts/middleware.md` |

**How to use:** After implementing a feature, walk through each row. For each artifact you created or modified, read the corresponding guide and verify the tests exist.

## 4. Artifact Type Testing Guide

When creating or modifying an artifact, read the corresponding guide for the complete recipe.

| Artifact Type | Pattern | Test Layer(s) | Guide |
|---|---|---|---|
| Entities | `*.entity.ts` | Integration (real DB) | `artifacts/entities.md` |
| Services | `*.service.ts` | Unit and/or Integration | `artifacts/services.md` |
| Modules | `*.module.ts` | Unit (compilation) | `artifacts/modules.md` |
| Controllers | `*.controller.ts` | E2E only | `artifacts/controllers.md` |
| DTOs | `*.dto.ts` | E2E (validation wiring) | `artifacts/dtos.md` |
| Guards | `*.guard.ts` | E2E or Unit+E2E | `artifacts/guards.md` |
| Strategies | `*.strategy.ts` | E2E (via guard) | `artifacts/strategies.md` |
| Pipes | `*.pipe.ts` | Unit | `artifacts/pipes.md` |
| Interceptors | `*.interceptor.ts` | Unit and/or E2E | `artifacts/interceptors.md` |
| Filters | `*.filter.ts` | Unit + E2E | `artifacts/filters.md` |
| Middleware | `*.middleware.ts` | E2E | `artifacts/middleware.md` |
| Future types | — | — | `artifacts/future-types.md` |

## 5. Anti-patterns — Do NOT Do This

- ❌ **Unit test controllers** — controllers are thin delegation layers; test via E2E only (see `artifacts/controllers.md`)
- ❌ **Mock configured libs** (JwtService, CacheManager, ThrottlerGuard) — use real instances with test config; mocking hides configuration bugs (§1)
- ❌ **Skip integration tests for services with DB access** — unit tests with mocked repos don't prove queries are correct (see `artifacts/services.md`)
- ❌ **Skip module compilation tests** — TypeScript catches type errors but cannot catch DI wiring errors; a missing import only fails at runtime (see `artifacts/modules.md`)
- ❌ **Use `repository.delete({})` for cleanup** — throws on empty criteria; use `dataSource.query('DELETE FROM table')` or `repository.clear()` (see `references/gotchas.md`)
- ❌ **Write mirror tests** — assertions that copy the implementation's return value prove nothing (§2)
- ❌ **Forget `afterAll(() => app.close())`** — causes Jest to hang on open handles (see `references/gotchas.md`)
- ❌ **Mock owned services' internals** — mock at the boundary (the injected service), not internal methods; if you need to mock `private` methods, the unit is too large (see `references/mock-health-rules.md`)
- ❌ **Skip reproducing `main.ts` global config in E2E** — `Test.createTestingModule()` does NOT execute `main.ts`; global pipes, filters, and interceptors must be applied explicitly (see `references/gotchas.md`)
- ❌ **Test every DTO rule individually** — one E2E test per endpoint proving `ValidationPipe` rejects bad input is enough; don't test `class-validator` internals (see `artifacts/dtos.md`)
- ❌ **Throw NestJS HTTP exceptions from services** — services throw domain exceptions; exception filters map them to HTTP responses (see `artifacts/filters.md`)

## 6. E2E Terminology Note

This guide uses "E2E" to mean **HTTP-layer integration tests** — tests that use supertest to exercise the full request-to-response chain (routing → guards → pipes → controller → service → response). This is NOT browser-based or multi-service end-to-end testing. Industry sources may call these "API integration tests" or "HTTP integration tests."

## 7. References

| Topic | File |
|---|---|
| External system strategies (DB, storage, queue, email) | `references/external-systems.md` |
| Mock health rules & boundary principle | `references/mock-health-rules.md` |
| File naming, directory structure, coverage targets | `references/file-conventions.md` |
| Stack-specific gotchas & pitfalls | `references/gotchas.md` |

## 8. How to Use This Guide

This guide is organized as a multi-file skill:
- **This file (SKILL.md)** — always loaded. Contains core rules, quick reference, and anti-patterns.
- **`artifacts/`** — one file per artifact type. Read the relevant file when creating or modifying that type.
- **`references/`** — supporting content. Read when you need details on mock strategies, file conventions, or gotchas.

When working on a feature:
1. Check §3 (Feature Implementation Checklist) to identify which artifacts need tests
2. Read the corresponding `artifacts/*.md` file for the complete testing recipe
3. Consult `references/` files as needed for mock strategies, conventions, or pitfalls

  ⎿  131	## @aws-sdk/client-s3
     132	
     133	**Source:** `/aws/aws-sdk-js-v3` (Context7), maps to `phase-03-videos/TD-03`, used by TD-05/06/07/08/12/13/15. **Pin:** `3.1152.0` (Node `>=20`). Keep it in lockstep with `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner`.
     134	
     135	### Client construction (MinIO + AWS)
     136	
     137	```ts
     138	import { S3Client } from '@aws-sdk/client-s3';
     139	
     140	const internal = new S3Client({
     141	  region: cfg.region,                 // e.g. 'us-east-1' (MinIO accepts any)
     142	  endpoint: cfg.endpoint,             // S3_ENDPOINT=http://minio:9000 (unset in prod)
     143	  forcePathStyle: cfg.forcePathStyle, // true for MinIO (v2's s3ForcePathStyle renamed)
     144	  credentials: { accessKeyId, secretAccessKey },
     145	  requestChecksumCalculation: 'WHEN_REQUIRED',
     146	  responseChecksumValidation: 'WHEN_REQUIRED',
     147	});
     148	// TD-05: a second instance, identical except endpoint = S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT, used ONLY for browser-facing presigning.
     149	```
     150	
     151	**Pitfall: flexible checksums.** The SDK default `requestChecksumCalculation: 'WHEN_SUPPORTED'` adds `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm` and `x-amz-checksum-crc32` to operations such as `PutObject` and `UploadPart`. In presigned URLs, those checksums would bind to a body the browser has not sent, and S3-compatible servers may reject them. Set both options to `'WHEN_REQUIRED'` on both clients (see aws-sdk-js-v3 issue #6810).
     152

     159	| Complete (TD-08) | `CompleteMultipartUploadCommand({ …, MultipartUpload: { Parts: [{ PartNumber, ETag }] } })` | Parts in ascending order. Take the ETags from `ListParts`, n … (+3427 chars)

# NestJS Best Practices

Comprehensive best practices guide for NestJS applications. Contains 40 rules across 10 categories, prioritized by impact to guide automated refactoring and code generation.

## When to Apply

Apply these guidelines during **planning and implementation** of NestJS features:

- **Planning phase:** When designing module architecture, defining service boundaries, or choosing patterns for a new feature in `nestjs-project/`
- **Implementation phase:** When writing or modifying controllers, services, guards, pipes, interceptors, entities, or repositories
- **Auth & Security:** When implementing authentication, authorization, guards, or input validation
- **Database:** When creating entities, repositories, migrations, or optimizing queries with TypeORM
- **Testing:** When writing unit or e2e tests for NestJS modules
- **Code Review:** When reviewing NestJS code for architecture, security, or performance issues

### When NOT to Apply

Do not load this skill when:

- Only reading or exploring NestJS code to understand it, without intent to plan or implement changes
- Working on frontend code (Next.js, React components) even if it consumes the NestJS API
- Answering general TypeScript or Node.js questions that don't involve NestJS framework patterns
- Discussing high-level project requirements without NestJS implementation scope

## Rule Categories by Priority

| Priority | Category | Impact | Prefix |
|----------|----------|--------|--------|
| 1 | Architecture | CRITICAL | `arch-` |
| 2 | Dependency Injection | CRITICAL | `di-` |
| 3 | Error Handling | HIGH | `error-` |
| 4 | Security | HIGH | `security-` |
| 5 | Performance | HIGH | `perf-` |
| 6 | Testing | MEDIUM-HIGH | `test-` |
| 7 | Database & ORM | MEDIUM-HIGH | `db-` |
| 8 | API Design | MEDIUM | `api-` |
| 9 | Microservices | MEDIUM | `micro-` |
| 10 | DevOps & Deployment | LOW-MEDIUM | `devops-` |

## Quick Reference

### 1. Architecture (CRITICAL)

- `arch-avoid-circular-deps` - Avoid circular module dependencies
- `arch-feature-modules` - Organize by feature, not technical layer
- `arch-module-sharing` - Proper module exports/imports, avoid duplicate providers
- `arch-single-responsibility` - Focused services over "god services"
- `arch-use-repository-pattern` - Abstract database logic for testability
- `arch-use-events` - Event-driven architecture for decoupling

### 2. Dependency Injection (CRITICAL)

- `di-avoid-service-locator` - Avoid service locator anti-pattern
- `di-interface-segregation` - Interface Segregation Principle (ISP)
- `di-liskov-substitution` - Liskov Substitution Principle (LSP)
- `di-prefer-constructor-injection` - Constructor over property injection
- `di-scope-awareness` - Understand singleton/request/transient scopes
- `di-use-interfaces-tokens` - Use injection tokens for interfaces

### 3. Error Handling (HIGH)

- `error-use-exception-filters` - Centralized exception handling
- `error-throw-http-exceptions` - Use NestJS HTTP exceptions
- `error-handle-async-errors` - Handle async errors properly

### 4. Security (HIGH)

- `security-auth-jwt` - Secure JWT authentication
- `security-validate-all-input` - Validate with class-validator
- `security-use-guards` - Authentication and authorization guards
- `security-sanitize-output` - Prevent XSS attacks
- `security-rate-limiting` - Implement rate limiting

### 5. Performance (HIGH)

- `perf-async-hooks` - Proper async lifecycle hooks
- `perf-use-caching` - Implement caching strategies
- `perf-optimize-database` - Optimize database queries
- `perf-lazy-loading` - Lazy load modules for faster startup

### 6. Testing (MEDIUM-HIGH)

- `test-use-testing-module` - Use NestJS testing utilities
- `test-e2e-supertest` - E2E testing with Supertest
- `test-mock-external-services` - Mock external dependencies

### 7. Database & ORM (MEDIUM-HIGH)

- `db-use-transactions` - Transaction management
- `db-avoid-n-plus-one` - Avoid N+1 query problems
- `db-use-migrations` - Use migrations for schema changes

### 8. API Design (MEDIUM)

- `api-use-dto-serialization` - DTO and response serialization
- `api-use-interceptors` - Cross-cutting concerns
- `api-versioning` - API versioning strategies
- `api-use-pipes` - Input transformation with pipes

### 9. Microservices (MEDIUM)

- `micro-use-patterns` - Message and event patterns
- `micro-use-health-checks` - Health checks for orchestration
- `micro-use-queues` - Background job processing

### 10. DevOps & Deployment (LOW-MEDIUM)

- `devops-use-config-module` - Environment configuration
- `devops-use-logging` - Structured logging
- `devops-graceful-shutdown` - Zero-downtime deployments

## How to Use

Read individual rule files for detailed explanations and code examples:

```
rules/arch-avoid-circular-deps.md
rules/security-validate-all-input.md
rules/_sections.md
```

Each rule file contains:
- Brief explanation of why it matters
- Incorrect code example with explanation
- Correct code example with explanation
- Additional context and references

● SI-03.4 is done: the storage service works end to end against the real MinIO. A presigned part URL accepts a `PUT` and answers `200` with an `ETag`, and a ranged read (`Range: bytes=0-99`) returns `206` with exactly 100 bytes. All 14 tests pass, and `tsc` and lint are clean.

**Created:**
- `src/storage/storage.constants.ts`
- `src/storage/s3-errors.ts`
- `src/storage/storage.module.ts`
- `src/storage/storage.service.ts`
- `src/storage/storage.module.spec.ts`
- `src/storage/storage.service.integration-spec.ts`

**Tests:** 14 passing (3 module unit, 11 integration against MinIO).

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Endpoints:** links for the browser start with `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT`, and the worker's read link starts with `S3_ENDPOINT`.
- **Link lifetime:** `X-Amz-Expires` follows the upload and delivery TTLs from config.
- **No checksum parameters:** part URLs don't contain `x-amz-checksum-crc32` or `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm`.
- **Downloads:** the requested `Content-Disposition` comes back on the response.
- **Missing things:** aborting an already-aborted upload doesn't fail, `headObject` on a missing key returns `null`, and `deletePrefix` removes everything under the prefix.

**Choices worth reviewing:**
- **Return shapes:** the two browser-facing signing methods return `{ url, expiresIn }`. `presignInternalGet` returns a plain string, because SI-03.15 hands it straight to ffprobe/ffmpeg.
- **Part order:** `completeMultipartUpload` sorts parts in ascending order itself, so callers can pass the `listParts` result directly.
- **Connections:** the service closes both S3 clients on shutdown, and Jest reports no open handles.
- **Testing guide:** it still says to test storage with a local-filesystem adapter. I followed the plan and tested against real MinIO; updating the guide is already part of SI-03.20.

  ⎿  31	## @nestjs/bullmq
     32	
     33	**Source:** `/nestjs/bull` (Context7), maps to `phase-03-videos/TD-01`. **Pin:** `12.0.0`, published 2026-08-27. Peer dependencies: `@nestjs/common` / `@nestjs/core` `^10 || ^11 || ^12` and `bullmq` `^3 … ^6`, so it works with Nest 11. It depends on `@nestjs/bull-shared@^12.0.0`.
     34	
     35	### Root connection (async, from the config namespace)
     36	
     37	```ts
     38	import { BullModule } from '@nestjs/bullmq';
     39	
     40	BullModule.forRootAsync({
     41	  imports: [ConfigModule],
     42	  inject: [queueConfig.KEY],
     43	  useFactory: (cfg: ConfigType<typeof queueConfig>) => ({
     44	    connection: { host: cfg.redisHost, port: cfg.redisPort }, // host = Compose service name "redis"
     45	  }),
     46	});
     47	```
     48	
     49	Follow the phase-01 convention: a `registerAs('queue', …)` factory validated by the Joi schema, injected through `ConfigType`.
     50	
     51	### Queue registration, producer, and consumer
     52	
     53	- `BullModule.registerQueue({ name: 'video-processing', defaultJobOptions: { attempts, backoff } })`. Any `QueueOptions` field is accepted. On `onApplicationShutdown`, the module closes workers and then the queue (`forceDisconnectOnShutdown` is optional).
     54	- Producer (API): `@InjectQueue('video-processing') private readonly queue: Queue`, then `queue.add(name, data, { jobId: videoId })`.
     55	- Consumer (worker only): the class **must** extend `WorkerHost`, or Nest throws `InvalidProcessorClassError`.
     56	
     57	```ts
     58	import { Processor, WorkerHost, OnWorkerEvent } from '@nestjs/bullmq';
     59	import { Job } from 'bullmq';
     60	
     61	@Processor('video-processing', { concurrency: 1 })
     62	export class VideoProcessingConsumer extends WorkerHost {
     63	  async process(job: Job<{ videoId: string }>): Promise<void> { /* … */ }
     64	
     65	  @OnWorkerEvent('failed')
     66	  onFailed(job: Job, err: Error) { /* … */ }
     67	}
     68	```
     69

- **The app is unaffected:** Node 25 loads them fine and `tsc` passes.

# TypeORM Development Guidelines

Expert guidance for TypeORM with TypeScript, focused on the Data Mapper pattern, PostgreSQL, and enterprise application architecture.

**Core principles:** explicit types on all columns, Data Mapper over Active Record, migrations over synchronize, environment-based configuration, and bidirectional relationships with explicit foreign keys.

## Rules Index

Rules are organized by priority. Load the relevant rule file when working on that topic.

### CRITICAL — Configuration

- `rules/config-datasource-setup.md` — DataSource with env vars, pooling, SSL. Never hardcode credentials.
- `rules/config-typescript-settings.md` — Required tsconfig flags for decorators and metadata.

### CRITICAL — Entity Design

- `rules/entity-define-proper-structure.md` — Explicit table names, typed columns, timestamps.
- `rules/entity-primary-key-strategy.md` — Auto-increment vs UUID vs composite PK by context.
- `rules/entity-column-types.md` — Explicit column types, enums, JSON, soft delete, versioning.

### HIGH — Relationships

- `rules/rel-one-to-one-and-many.md` — Bidirectional OneToOne/OneToMany with JoinColumn and FK.
- `rules/rel-many-to-many.md` — ManyToMany with explicit JoinTable configuration.

### HIGH — Query Patterns

- `rules/query-repository-basics.md` — Repository CRUD: create, save, find, delete, softDelete.
- `rules/query-custom-repository.md` — Custom repositories for domain-specific queries.
- `rules/query-builder-and-n-plus-one.md` — QueryBuilder usage and N+1 prevention.

### HIGH — Migrations

- `rules/migration-workflow.md` — CLI commands, workflow, and why synchronize must be false.
- `rules/migration-file-structure.md` — Reversible migrations with proper up/down methods.

### MEDIUM-HIGH — Transactions

- `rules/tx-use-transactions.md` — QueryRunner and transaction callback for multi-entity writes.

### MEDIUM — Integration

- `rules/integ-nestjs-setup.md` — NestJS forRoot/forFeature setup with DI.

### MEDIUM — Conventions

- `rules/conv-eager-lazy-loading.md` — Explicit loading over eager/lazy; when to use each.
- `rules/conv-indexes-naming-cascades.md` — Indexes, SnakeNamingStrategy, cascade operations.

## Quick Reference

| Topic | Key Rule |
|-------|----------|
| New entity | Always: explicit table name, typed columns, timestamps |
| New relation | Always: bidirectional, @JoinColumn on owning side, explicit FK |
| New query | Prefer Repository API; use QueryBuilder for complex joins |
| Schema change | Generate migration, review SQL, implement down() |
| Multi-entity write | Wrap in transaction (QueryRunner or callback) |
| NestJS setup | forRoot() at app level, forFeature() per module |

## Metadata

- **Category index:** `rules/_sections.md`
- **Rule template:** `rules/_template.md`
- **Total rules:** 16 across 8 categories

     ---
     paths:
       - 'nestjs-project/**/migrations/**'
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*data-source.ts'
     description: 'Database migration safety rules'
     ---
     
     # Migration Rules
     
     ## Immutability
     
     - Never edit a migration that has already been executed — create a new one instead
     - If a migration needs to be reverted, write a new migration that undoes the change
     
     ## Generation
     
     - Always generate migrations via TypeORM CLI (`typeorm migration:generate` or `typeorm migration:create`)
     - Never write migration SQL by hand unless the CLI cannot express the change (e.g., data migrations)
     
     ## Safety
     
     - Never use `synchronize: true` in any environment — migrations are the only sanctioned way to change the schema
     - Test migrations against a fresh database before considering them done
     - Migrations must be idempotent where possible — use `IF EXISTS` / `IF NOT EXISTS` guards for DDL
     
     ## Recovering from `synchronize` Residue
     
     If you find entities in `src/` whose tables already exist in the database but have **no corresponding migration file on disk**, the database was previously populated by `synchronize: true` (or by a deleted migration). Do **not** try to "patch" the schema with a new migration on top — the generated diff will be empty and TypeORM will think everything is fine while the migration table is wrong.
     
     Correct recovery:
     
     1. Drop the orphan tables in the dev database (`DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`).
     2. Clear the `migrations` table if it has stale rows.
     3. Run `typeorm migration:generate` against the empty database — it will now produce a complete `CREATE TABLE` migration that matches the entities.
     4. Run the new migration to recreat … (+3388 chars)

     ---
     paths:
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.entity.ts'
     description: 'TypeORM entity conventions for database models'
     ---
     
     # Entity Rules
     
     ## Naming and Structure
     
     - Always pass an explicit table name to `@Entity('table_name')` — do not rely on auto-generated names
     - Use UUID as primary key: `@PrimaryGeneratedColumn('uuid')`
     - Always include timestamp columns: `@CreateDateColumn()` and `@UpdateDateColumn()`
     
     ## Column Conventions
     
     - Sensitive fields (passwords, tokens) must use `{ select: false }` to exclude from default queries
     - Use `{ unique: true }` for naturally unique fields (email, slug)
     - Define explicit column types when the default mapping is ambiguous
     
     ## Relationships
     
     - Always define both sides of a relationship (e.g., `@OneToMany` + `@ManyToOne`)
     - Prefer explicit relation loading (`relations: [...]`) — load only what each query needs
     - Use eager loading only for low-cardinality, always-needed relations (e.g., `user.role`)
     - Use lazy loading only when the relation is rarely accessed and the `Promise<>` type is acceptable
     
     ## Schema Changes
     
     - Never modify an entity without creating a corresponding migration
     - Never use `synchronize: true` in production — only in early development if at all
     - Generate migrations via TypeORM CLI, not by hand

**Decisions for you:**
- **No inverse relation on `Channel`:** the rule file `nestjs-entities.md` asks for both sides of every relation, but the plan explicitly says not to add `Channel.videos` in this phase. I followed the plan.
- **No database check on `declared_size`:** the data model gives it the limits "> 0, ≤ 10 GiB", but the plan's migration checklist has no CHECK constraint, so the limit is enforced only by the API (SI-03.8). Adding a database-level check would be a separate task.
- **Empty databases:** the test cleanup helper always deletes from `videos`. On a brand-new empty database, suites that don't create that table would fail. That's the same assumption the existing tables already make, so I logged it as a separate task.

     import { Injectable } from '@nestjs/common';
     import { DataSource, QueryFailedError } from 'typeorm';
     import { appendRandomSuffix, sanitizeNickname } from './nickname.util';
     import { Channel } from './entities/channel.entity';
     
     const PG_UNIQUE_VIOLATION = '23505';
     const NICKNAME_COLUMN = 'nickname';
     const MAX_RETRIES = 5;
     
     interface PgErrorFields {
       code?: unknown;
       detail?: unknown;
     }
     
     function isPgUniqueViolationOnColumn(err: unknown, column: string): boolean {
       if (!(err instanceof QueryFailedError)) return false;
       const e = err as PgErrorFields;
       return (
         e.code === PG_UNIQUE_VIOLATION &&
         typeof e.detail === 'string' &&
         e.detail.includes(column)
       );
     }
     
     @Injectable()
     export class ChannelsService {
       constructor(private readonly dataSource: DataSource) {}
     
       async createChannel(userId: string, email: string): Promise<Channel> {
         const baseNickname = sanitizeNickname(email.split('@')[0]);
     
         return this.dataSource.transaction(async (manager) => {
           let nickname = baseNickname;
     
           for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= MAX_RETRIES; attempt++) {
             const existing = await manager.findOne(Channel, {
               where: { nickname },
             });
             if (existing) {
               nickname = appendRandomSuffix(baseNickname);
               continue;
             }
     
             try {
               return await manager.save(
                 manager.create(Channel, {
                   name: baseNickname,
                   nickname,
                   user_id: userId,
                 }),
               );
             } catch (err) {
               if (isPgUniqueViolationOnColumn(err, NICKNAME_COLUMN)) {
                 // Concurrent insert between pre-check and save — retry with new suffix
                 nickname = appendRandomSuffix(baseNickname);
               } else {
                 throw err;
               }
             }
           }
     
           throw new Error(
             'Nickname conflict could not be resolved after max retries',
           );
         });
       }
     }

**Tests:** 27 passing.

**What the acceptance criteria now guarantee:**
- **Slug collisions:** a colliding slug is retried with a new one without the caller noticing. A collision on any other column is re-raised immediately.
- **Ownership:** another channel's video and a missing id both return `VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Status changes:** a second `markProcessing` returns `false`, and `markFailed(..., draft)` leaves an already-`processing` video untouched.
- **Cleanup finders:** they respect the time thresholds and the limit.

**Choices worth reviewing:**
- **After 5 slug collisions:** `createDraft` throws a plain `Error`, like the existing channel-nickname retry. The Error Catalog has no code for this, so it would come back as a 500.
- **Processing start time:** `processing_started_at` is set with the database's `now()`, so it uses the same clock as `created_at`, which the cleanup thresholds compare against.
- **Loose duplicate check:** the shared check finds the column name anywhere in the error text, so a value that happens to contain "slug" could trigger it. I kept it as is, since the plan says no behaviour change; tightening it is logged as a separate task.

  ⎿  713	### API Contracts
     714	
     715	Todos os endpoints abaixo exigem `Authorization: Bearer <access_token>` (guard global `JwtAuthGuard`, `phase-02-auth/TD-02`) e são servidos por `VideosController` (`@ApiTags('videos')`, `@ApiBearerAuth('access-token')`, `@SkipThrottle()` — o rate limit de `phase-02-auth/TD-08` é escopado ao fluxo de auth e não pode bloquear o envio de lotes de partes). Erros seguem o envelope `{ statusCode, error, message }` (`phase-02-auth/TD-07`) e são documentados com `@ApiResponse({ schema: { $ref: getSchemaPath(ApiErrorEnvelope) } })` (`openapi-docs-nestjs/TD-01`). Um `:id` que não é UUID retorna `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` (`ParseUUIDPipe`). Um vídeo inexistente ou de outro canal retorna `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND` em **todos** os endpoints com `:id` (existência não vaza — per `phase-03-videos/TD-15`).
     716

     718

     721	- title: string
     722	- original_filename: string
     723	- mime_type: string — `video/mp4` | `video/webm`
     724	- status: string — `draft` | `processing` | `ready` | `failed`
     725	- failure_reason: string | null — ver Data Model → Failure reasons
     726	- duration: number | null — segundos
     727	- width: integer | null
     728	- height: integer | null
     729	- size: integer | null — bytes
     730	- metadata: object | null — `{ video_codec, audio_codec, format_name, bit_rate, frame_rate }`
     731	- has_thumbnail: boolean
     732	- created_at: string (ISO-8601)
     733	- updated_at: string (ISO-8601)

     910	| `title` | opcional; string 1..100 após trim | DTO (`@IsOptional`, `@IsString`, `@Length(1, 100)`, `@Transform` trim) |
     911	| `part_numbers` | array 1..100, únicos, inteiros ≥ 1; cada um ≤ `part_count` | DTO (`@IsArray`, `@ArrayMinSize(1)`, `@ArrayMaxSize(100)`, `@ArrayUnique`, `@IsInt({ each: true })`, `@Min(1, { each: true })`) + `VideoUploadsService` (`INVALID_PART_NUMBER`) |
     912	| `:id` | UUID | `ParseUUIDPipe` |

     # POST /videos — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     `POST /videos` inicia o upload de um vídeo de até 10 GiB: valida o formato (allowlist `video/mp4` / `video/webm`, extensão de `file_name` compatível com `mime_type`) e o tamanho declarado, abre o multipart upload direto no storage em `videos/{videoId}/original` e pré-cadastra automaticamente o vídeo como `draft` no canal do usuário autenticado, devolvendo o `VideoResponseDto` acrescido do plano de partes (`upload.part_size` fixo de 64 MiB e `upload.part_count = ceil(size / part_size)`). Os bytes do vídeo nunca passam pela API. O endpoint exige JWT (guard global) e é `@SkipThrottle()` — o rate limit de auth não se aplica.
     
     ## Test Scenarios
     
     ### 1. Iniciar upload e pré-cadastrar o rascunho
     
     **Setup:** `beforeAll` — `Test.createTestingModule({ imports: [AppModule] }).compile()` + `createNestApplication()` reproduzindo a config global de `main.ts` (`ValidationPipe({ whitelist, forbidNonWhitelisted, transform })`, `DomainExceptionFilter`, `ValidationExceptionFilter`), contra Postgres, MinIO e Redis reais (`QUEUE_PREFIX=streamtube-test`, `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT=http://minio:9000` via `src/test/setup-test-env.ts`); `beforeEach` — `cleanAllTables(dataSource)` + usuário autenticado (com canal) via `createAuthenticatedUser(app)` de `test/helpers/auth.helper.ts` (extraído neste SI — ação técnica 6; a suíte de auth continua passando sem mudança de comportamento); `afterAll` — `StorageService.deletePrefix(videoPrefix(id))` para cada vídeo criado + `app.close()`.

     async function bootstrap() {
       const app = await NestFactory.create(AppModule);
       const configService = app.get(ConfigService);
       const port = configService.get<number>('app.port') ?? 3000;
     
       app.useGlobalPipes(
         new ValidationPipe({
           whitelist: true,
           forbidNonWhitelisted: true,
           transform: true,
         }),
       );
     
       app.useGlobalFilters(
         new DomainExceptionFilter(),
         new ValidationExceptionFilter(),
       );
     
       const swagger = app.get<ConfigType<typeof swaggerConfig>>(swaggerConfig.KEY);
     
       if (swagger.enabled) {
         await SwaggerModule.loadPluginMetadata(swaggerMetadata);
         const document = buildSwaggerDocument(app);
         SwaggerModule.setup('api/docs', app, document, {
           customSiteTitle: 'StreamTube API Docs',
           swaggerOptions: { persistAuthorization: true },
         });
       }
     
       await app.listen(port);
     }

  ⎿  const mailpitUrl = `http://${process.env.MAIL_HOST ?? 'mailpit'}:8025`;
     
     export async function getMailpitMessages(): Promise<any[]> {
       const res = await fetch(`${mailpitUrl}/api/v1/messages`);
       const data = (await res.json()) as { messages: any[] };
       return data.messages ?? [];
     }
     
     export async function getMailpitMessage(id: string): Promise<any> {
       const res = await fetch(`${mailpitUrl}/api/v1/message/${id}`);
       return res.json();
     }
     
     export async function clearMailpitMessages(): Promise<void> {
       await fetch(`${mailpitUrl}/api/v1/messages`, { method: 'DELETE' });
     }

● Bash(cd /Users/ricardorauberpereira/Downloads/MBA-Desafios/Upload_Processamento_Videos-StreamTube && cat .claude/rules/nestjs-layer-separation.md .claude/rules/nestjs-controllers.md .claude/rules/nestjs-dtos.md)
  ⎿  ---
     paths:
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.controller.ts'
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.guard.ts'
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.interceptor.ts'
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.pipe.ts'
       - 'nestjs-project/**/*.filter.ts'
     description: 'Layer separation — business logic belongs exclusively in services; controllers, guards, interceptors, pipes, and filters must delegate to services'
     ---
     
     # Layer Separation Rules
     
     ## Business Logic Lives in Services
     
     Services are the single home for all business rules and core domain logic. No other artifact type may implement business logic directly.
     
     ## Controllers Are Thin
     
     Controllers receive HTTP requests, delegate to services, and return the response. They must not contain conditionals, transformations, or decisions that encode business rules.
     
     Controllers **may** contain simple request-level decisions that are not domain logic — such as checking if a service returned `null` and throwing an appropriate exception, or deciding which service to call based on a query parameter. These are HTTP-layer concerns, not business rules.
     
     ## Guards, Interceptors, Pipes, and Filters Must Delegate
     
     When a Guard, Interceptor, Pipe, or Exception Filter needs access to business logic, it must inject and call a Service. These artifacts handle cross-cutting infrastructure concerns (authentication, logging, validation, error mapping) — not domain decisions.
     
     ### Bad: business logic in a guard
     
     ```typescript
     @Injectable()
     export class ChannelOwnerGuard implements CanActivate {
       canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {
         const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
         const channel = request.channel;
         // business rule leaked into guard
         return channel.ownerId === request.user.id && channel.status === 'active';
       }
     }
     ```
     
     ### Good: guard delegates to a service
     
     ```typescript
     @Injectable()
     export class ChannelOwnerGuard implements CanActivate {
       constructor(private readonly channelsService: ChannelsService) {}
     
       async canActivate(context: ExecutionCon … (+9777 chars)

● Bash(sed -n 20,60p test/helpers/auth.helper.ts)
  ⎿  /** Registers the user and returns the raw confirmation token instead of mailing it. */
     export async function captureConfirmationToken(
       app: INestApplication<App>,
       email: string,
       password = DEFAULT_PASSWORD,
     ): Promise<string> {
       const authService = app.get(AuthService);
       const mailServiceInstance = (authService as any).mailService;
       let capturedToken = '';
       jest
         .spyOn(mailServiceInstance, 'sendConfirmationEmail')
         .mockImplementationOnce((_e: string, _n: string, t: string) => {
           capturedToken = t;
           return Promise.resolve();
         });
       await request(app.getHttpServer())
         .post('/auth/register')
         .send({ email, password });
       return capturedToken;
     }
     
     export async function registerConfirmAndLogin(
       app: INestApplication<App>,
       email: string,
       password = DEFAULT_PASSWORD,
     ): Promise<AuthTokens> {
       const token = await captureConfirmationToken(app, email, password);
       await request(app.getHttpServer())
         .get('/auth/confirm-email')
         .query({ token });
       const res = await request(app.getHttpServer())
         .post('/auth/login')
         .send({ email, password });
       return {
         access_token: res.body.access_token,
         refresh_token: res.body.refresh_token,
       };
     }
     
     /**

**Tests:** all 19 unit/integration tests pass and all 7 new e2e tests pass. The full e2e suite passes (59), and so do 226 of the unit/integration tests; the only one left out is the migrations spec. `tsc` and lint are clean.

**Fixes made during this SI:**
- **Missing `User` entity (fix attempt 1):** the integration test failed with `Entity metadata for Channel#user was not found`. `ChannelsModule` registers `Channel`, but its link to `User` only resolves when `UsersModule` is also loaded, as it is in the full app. I made the test register `User` the same way. The worker in SI-03.16 builds this smaller module set and will hit the same error.
- **`test:e2e` wasn't running in band:** `nestjs-project/CLAUDE.md` says it already did, but it didn't. With the new suite, which wipes shared tables, all 4 e2e suites failed when run in parallel. With `--runInBand` all 59 pass.

**Notes for the next SIs:**
- **Auth helper:** `createAuthenticatedUser` returns `{ userId, email, access_token }` and uses a unique email on every call, since later specs need two users per test.
- **Rate limit:** the helper makes 3 auth requests against the 10-per-minute limit, so each video e2e suite must clear the throttler storage in `beforeEach`. The test plans for SI-03.9 to SI-03.13 don't mention this.

**Decision for you:** `migrations.integration-spec.ts` (from SI-03.6) can deadlock.
- **Cause:** its setup drops tables in parallel. Since `videos` references `channels`, dropping both at the same time can fail with `deadlock detected`.
- **What it broke:** it passed when run alone in SI-03.6, but in the full `npm test` run it deadlocked. The shared database was left with only a stray `channels` table and an empty `migrations` table, and 14 suites failed after it.
- **Repair:** I dropped the stray table (one test-fixture row) and the two enum types, then re-ran `migration:run`. All 3 migrations are applied again.
- **Not fixed yet:** it's a completed SI's test, so I didn't edit it quietly.
- **My recommendation:** a small bugfix before continuing that runs the drops one at a time, or as a single `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS a, b, … CASCADE` statement. Without it, the full test suite in final verification can fail at random.

Seguir para SI-03.14?

     201	
     202	- **Metadata:** `ffprobe -v error -print_format json -show_format -show_streams <url>`. Read `format.format_name`, `format.duration`, `format.size` and `format.bit_rate`. Per stream, read `codec_type`, `codec_name`, `width`, `height` and `avg_frame_rate` (a fraction such as `"30000/1001"`). A non-zero exit code or empty `streams` means a permanent error, which maps to `UnrecoverableError`.
     203	- **Thumbnail (AMB-3 rule):** `ffmpeg -v error -ss <t> -i <url> -frames:v 1 -vf "scale='min(1280,iw)':-2" -f image2 -c:v mjpeg pipe:1` (or write to a temp file). Placing `-ss` **before** `-i` makes it an input seek: ffmpeg jumps to the nearest seek point, then decodes forward. Here `t = 0.1 * duration`, or 0 when the duration is under 1 s.
     204	- **HTTP input (TD-12):** ffmpeg's HTTP protocol issues Range requests for seekable inputs. Resilience options include `-reconnect 1`, `-reconnect_on_network_error 1`, `-reconnect_on_http_error 5xx`, `-reconnect_delay_max <s>` and `-reconnect_max_retries <n>`. Network failures should surface as retryable errors (no `UnrecoverableError`).
     205	- **Test fixtures:** generate clips at test time with `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc=duration=3:size=320x240:rate=25 -f lavfi -i sine=frequency=440:duration=3 -c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p -c:a aac -shortest out.mp4`. For WebM, use `-c:v libvpx-vp9 -c:a libopus`. Corrupt or unsupported inputs can be built from truncated bytes or `-c:v mpeg4` in an `.avi` container.
     206	- Invoke the binaries with `execFile`/`spawn` and an argument array (no shell), plus a timeout and stderr capture, behind an injectable service (TD-11).
     207

**What the tests confirm:**
- **MP4 (H.264/AAC):** 320x240, 25 fps, about 3 seconds, `format_name` containing `mp4`.
- **WebM and silent MP4:** the WebM reads as `vp9`/`opus`, and an MP4 without audio gives `audio_codec: null`.
- **Through storage:** `probe` works directly on a presigned MinIO URL, and so does frame capture.
- **Frame sizes:** a 1920x1080 video gives a 1280x720 JPEG, and a 320x240 video stays 320x240. The size is checked by running ffprobe on the JPEG itself.
- **Errors:** a corrupt file is `invalid_media`, and an unreachable storage URL is `transient`.

**Notes for SI-03.15:**
- **Field names:** the result uses the same snake_case names as the video metadata (`video_codec`, `audio_codec`, `frame_rate`, …), so it maps straight into `markReady`.
- **Missing duration:** `duration` can be `null`, because ffprobe leaves it out for some inputs (the JPEG check above is one). SI-03.15 has to decide what a missing duration means.
- **Empty frame:** if ffmpeg exits successfully but produces no image, `extractFrame` raises `invalid_media`, so an empty thumbnail is never stored.

**Decision for you:** a 404 from storage (the object is missing) counts as `transient`, following the plan's rule that anything unrecognised gets retried. The worker would therefore retry until it runs out of attempts and then mark the video `processing_error`. If you'd rather a missing object fail immediately as permanent, that's a plan change.

     # GET /videos/:id — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     `GET /videos/:id` expõe ao dono a leitura mínima de um vídeo em qualquer status (`draft`, `processing`, `ready`, `failed`), permitindo acompanhar o processamento por polling e obter o `slug` que forma a URL única do vídeo. A posse é resolvida pelo canal (`channels.user_id = sub`); um vídeo inexistente ou de outro canal retorna o mesmo `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`, sem vazar existência. Um `:id` que não é UUID é rejeitado pelo `ParseUUIDPipe` com `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`.

  ⎿  import {
       ArgumentsHost,
       BadRequestException,
       Catch,
       ExceptionFilter,
     } from '@nestjs/common';
     import { Response } from 'express';
     
     @Catch(BadRequestException)
     export class ValidationExceptionFilter implements ExceptionFilter {
       catch(exception: BadRequestException, host: ArgumentsHost): void {
         const response = host.switchToHttp().getResponse<Response>();
         const exceptionResponse = exception.getResponse() as Record<
           string,
           unknown
         >;
     
         const raw = exceptionResponse['message'];
         const message = Array.isArray(raw) ? raw : [raw];
     
         response.status(400).json({
           statusCode: 400,
           error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
           message,
         });
       }
     }

  ⎿  # GET /videos/:id/upload — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     `GET /videos/:id/upload` permite ao cliente retomar um upload interrompido sem guardar estado próprio: o endpoint consulta o storage (`ListParts` do multipart aberto em `videos/{videoId}/original`) e devolve `part_size`, `part_count` (derivado do tamanho declarado) e as `uploaded_parts` já armazenadas, ordenadas por `part_number`. Só o dono acessa (não dono → `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`) e somente com o vídeo em `draft` (senão `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`).
     
     ## Test Scenarios
     
     ### 1. Consultar o estado do upload para retomada
     
     **Setup:** `beforeAll` — `Test.createTestingModule({ imports: [AppModule] }).compile()` + `createNestApplication()` reproduzindo a config global de `main.ts` (`ValidationPipe({ whitelist, forbidNonWhitelisted, transform })`, `DomainExceptionFilter`, `ValidationExceptionFilter`), contra Postgres e MinIO reais (`S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT=http://minio:9000` via `src/test/setup-test-env.ts`, para que as URLs pré-assinadas sejam alcançáveis de dentro do container); `beforeEach` — `cleanAllTables(dataSource)` + dois usuários autenticados (dono e outro), cada um com canal, via `createAuthenticatedUser(app)` de `test/helpers/auth.helper.ts` (extraído em SI-03.8); o vídeo do dono é criado via `POST /videos` com `{ "file_name": "clip.mp4", "mime_type": "video/mp4", "size": 134217729 }` (`part_count: 3`); `afterAll` — `StorageService.deletePrefix(videoPrefix(id))` dos vídeos criados + `app.close()`.

     100	  // reconciliation sweep is the safety net for anything left behind.
     101	  private async abortQuietly(key: string, uploadId: string): Promise<void> {
     102	    try {
     103	      await this.storageService.abortMultipartUpload(key, uploadId);
     104	    } catch (abortErr) {
     105	      this.logger.warn(
     106	        `Failed to abort multipart upload ${uploadId} for ${key}: ${String(abortErr)}`,
     107	      );
     108	    }
     109	  }

● SI-03.10 is done: `GET /videos/:id/upload` returns the part plan and the parts already in storage, so an interrupted upload can resume. All 32 tests pass (28 unit/integration and 4 e2e), no multipart uploads were left in MinIO, and `tsc` and lint are clean.

**Created:** `src/videos/dto/upload-status-response.dto.ts`, `test/videos-upload-status.e2e-spec.ts`

**Updated:**
- `src/videos/video-uploads.service.ts`: adds `getUploadStatus`, plus a private check that verifies ownership before the `draft` status, so a non-owner always gets 404, never 409.
- `src/videos/videos.controller.ts`: adds `GET :id/upload` with the full OpenAPI annotations.
- `src/videos/video-uploads.service.spec.ts`: 10 new unit cases.
- `src/videos/video-uploads.service.integration-spec.ts`: 1 new case, and the cleanup fix below.

**Tests:** 32 passing.

**What the tests confirm:**
- **New draft:** returns `{ part_size: 67108864, part_count: 3, uploaded_parts: [] }`.
- **After uploading parts 1 and 3:** returns exactly those two parts, in order, with the sizes sent (5 MiB and 1 MiB).
- **Video already processing:** returns `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`.
- **Non-owner:** returns `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`.

**Things to know:**
- **Deviation from the test plan:** the plan gets the part URLs from `POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls`, but that endpoint only arrives in SI-03.11. The e2e test signs the two URLs directly with `StorageService`, using the same client the endpoint will use. That step can switch to the endpoint once SI-03.11 is in.
- **Leak fixed in an SI-03.8 test file:** the integration test's cleanup looked up each upload id in the database after `beforeEach` had already wiped the rows. With the second test added here, the first test's multipart upload would never have been closed. Upload ids are now recorded when each video is created.

● Read(/Users/ricardorauberpereira/Downloads/MBA-Desafios/Upload_Processamento_Videos-StreamTube/docs/phases/phase-03-videos/phase-03-videos.md)
  ⎿  801	#### POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls (SI-03.11)
     802	
     803	Gera URLs pré-assinadas de `UploadPart` (assinadas com o cliente **público**, `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT` — per `phase-03-videos/TD-05`) para um lote de partes; o browser faz `PUT` de cada parte direto no storage (per `phase-03-videos/TD-07`). TTL = `S3_UPLOAD_PART_URL_TTL_SECONDS` (default 3600).
     804	
     805	**Request headers:**
     806	- Content-Type: application/json
     807	- Authorization: Bearer <access_token>
     808	
     809	**Request body:**
     810	- part_numbers: integer[], required — 1..100 itens, únicos, cada um ≥ 1 e ≤ `part_count`
     811	
     812	**Response 200:**
     813	- expires_in: integer — segundos de validade das URLs
     814	- parts: array of `{ part_number: integer, url: string }` — na mesma ordem de `part_numbers`
     815	
     816	**Error responses:**
     817	- 400 VALIDATION_ERROR: corpo inválido (array vazio, > 100 itens, duplicados, não inteiros, < 1) ou `:id` não é UUID
     818	- 400 INVALID_PART_NUMBER: algum `part_number` > `part_count`
     819	- 401 Unauthorized: sem token / token inválido
     820	- 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND: vídeo inexistente ou de outro canal
     821	- 409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS: vídeo não está em `draft`
     822	
     823	---
     824	
● Bash(cd /Users/ricardorauberpereira/Downloads/MBA-Desafios/Upload_Processamento_Videos-StreamTube/nestjs-project && sed -n 9,200p specs/videos-upload-part-urls.plan.md)
  ⎿  # POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     `POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls` entrega ao cliente, em lotes de até 100 partes, as URLs pré-assinadas de `UploadPart` (assinadas com o cliente **público**, `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT`) para enviar cada parte de 64 MiB direto ao storage — o plano de dados do upload de 10 GiB não passa pela API. A resposta traz `expires_in` (TTL configurável por `S3_UPLOAD_PART_URL_TTL_SECONDS`, default 3600) e `parts` na mesma ordem de `part_numbers`. Só o dono acessa (não dono → `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`) e somente com o vídeo em `draft` (senão `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`); números acima de `part_count` retornam `400 INVALID_PART_NUMBER`.
     
     ## Test Scenarios
     
     ### 1. Gerar URLs pré-assinadas de partes
     
     **Setup:** `beforeAll` — `Test.createTestingModule({ imports: [AppModule] }).compile()` + `createNestApplication()` reproduzindo a config global de `main.ts` (`ValidationPipe({ whitelist, forbidNonWhitelisted, transform })`, `DomainExceptionFilter`, `ValidationExceptionFilter`), contra Postgres e MinIO reais (`S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT=http://minio:9000` via `src/test/setup-test-env.ts`); `beforeEach` — `cleanAllTables(dataSource)` + dois usuários autenticados (dono e outro), cada um com canal, via `createAuthenticatedUser(app)` de `test/helpers/auth.helper.ts` (extraído em SI-03.8); o vídeo do dono é criado via `POST /videos` com `{ "file_name": "clip.mp4", "mime_type": "video/mp4", "size": 134217729 }` (`part_count: 3`); `afterAll` — `StorageService.deletePrefix(videoPrefix(id))` dos vídeos criados + `app.close()`.

● SI-03.11 is done. `POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls` hands out a batch of presigned URLs, and the client uploads each part straight to storage with them. All 47 tests pass (37 unit/integration, 10 e2e), no multipart uploads were left in MinIO, and `tsc`, lint and Prettier are clean.

**Created:**
- `src/videos/dto/create-part-urls.dto.ts` (1 to 100 unique integers, each at least 1)
- `src/videos/dto/part-urls-response.dto.ts`
- `test/videos-upload-part-urls.e2e-spec.ts`

**Updated:**
- `src/videos/video-uploads.service.ts`: adds `presignParts`.
- `src/videos/videos.controller.ts`: adds `POST :id/upload/part-urls` (returns `200`) with the full OpenAPI annotations.
- `video-uploads.service.spec.ts` and `video-uploads.service.integration-spec.ts`: new test cases.
- `test/videos-upload-status.e2e-spec.ts`: scenario `lista-partes-ja-enviadas` now gets its URLs from the `part-urls` endpoint instead of signing them directly, as you asked, so it matches the original test plan.

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Default response:** `expires_in: 3600`, the URLs start with `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT`, and they come back in the requested order (`[3, 1]` stays `[3, 1]`).
- **Storage accepts them:** a 5 MiB `PUT` to a returned URL gets `200` with an `ETag`, and the part then shows up in `GET /videos/:id/upload`.
- **Shorter lifetime:** with `S3_UPLOAD_PART_URL_TTL_SECONDS=600`, the response has `expires_in: 600` and the URL carries `X-Amz-Expires=600`. This test runs on a second app instance started with that setting.
- **Part number out of range:** asking for a part above the video's part count returns `400 INVALID_PART_NUMBER`.
- **Bad batches:** an empty list, 101 items, duplicates, or `0` each return `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`.
- **Wrong caller or status:** a non-owner gets `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`, and a video that isn't a draft returns `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`.

The ownership and draft checks run before the part-range check, so a non-owner still gets 404 even when asking for an invalid part.

● SI-03.15 is done: `VideoProcessingService` checks that an uploaded video plays in the browser, reads its metadata, stores a thumbnail, and marks it `ready`. All 52 tests pass, and the integration tests ran against real Postgres, MinIO and FFmpeg. `tsc` and lint are clean, and nothing was left behind in the temp folder or in MinIO.

**Created:**
- `src/video-processing/video-processing-errors.ts`
- `src/video-processing/playback-allowlist.ts`
- `src/video-processing/video-processing.service.ts`
- `src/video-processing/video-processing.module.ts` (no queue consumer; it exports `VideoProcessingService` for the worker)
- tests: `playback-allowlist.spec.ts`, `video-processing.service.spec.ts`, `video-processing.service.integration-spec.ts`

**Updated:** `src/video-processing/media/probe-parser.ts` and its spec, to apply your duration rule (details below).

**Tests:** 52 passing, after one fix to a test (below).

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Valid MP4 (H.264/AAC):** becomes `ready` with duration, width, height, the stored file size, the five metadata fields and `has_thumbnail = true`.
- **Thumbnail:** saved as `thumbnail.jpg` with `Content-Type: image/jpeg`; a 1920x1080 video gets a 1280-px-wide thumbnail.
- **Valid WebM (VP9/Opus):** also becomes `ready`.
- **Thumbnail position:** taken at 0 s for clips under 1 second, and at 10% of the duration otherwise (1 s for a 10 s clip).
- **Rejected formats:** MPEG-4 Part 2, or a WebM declared as `video/mp4`, fails with `unsupported_format` and no thumbnail is saved.
- **Corrupt file:** fails with `invalid_media`.
- **Reprocessing:** running it again on a `ready` video changes neither the database row nor the thumbnail.

**Your duration decision, applied:** if the container has no duration, the parser now uses the video stream's duration. If neither has one, processing fails permanently with `invalid_media`, before any thumbnail is made.

**Things to know:**
- **Frame-extraction failures:** a broken frame now also fails permanently with `invalid_media`. The plan only said this for the metadata read, but a frame that can't be decoded won't fix itself on retry.
- **Errors that stay retryable:** temporary FFmpeg errors and storage errors are passed on unchanged, so the queue retries them.
- **Where a failure gets recorded:** this service only raises errors. Recording a failed video as `failed` belongs to the queue consumer in SI-03.16.
- **Format check:** the container type must match exactly one of the names ffprobe lists (e.g. `mp4`, `webm`), not just appear somewhere in the text.
- **The test fix:** the integration test sent `HEAD` requests to a presigned link, and MinIO rejected them with 403 because the link only allows GET. The test now does a 1-byte GET instead; the service code didn't change.
- **Worker heads-up (SI-03.16):** the integration test again had to register `User` by hand, because the `Channel` → `User` link isn't loaded through this module chain. The worker in SI-03.16 builds exactly this chain, so it will need a fix there.

Idempotente: chamado de novo com o vídeo em `processing` ou `ready`, devolve `200` com o estado atual sem efeitos colaterais.

     830	- Authorization: Bearer <access_token>

     837	- 400 VALIDATION_ERROR: `:id` não é UUID
     838	- 401 Unauthorized: sem token / token inválido
     839	- 404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND: vídeo inexistente ou de outro canal
     840	- 409 UPLOAD_INCOMPLETE: faltam partes (`1..part_count` não contíguo) ou a soma dos tamanhos das partes ≠ `declared_size`; o vídeo continua em `draft` e o upload pode ser retomado
     841	- 409 UPLOAD_SIZE_MISMATCH: o objeto montado tem tamanho ≠ `declared_size` (ou > 10 GiB); o objeto é removido e o vídeo vai para `failed` com `size_mismatch`
     842	- 409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS: vídeo em `failed` (inclusive abandonado pelo sweep durante a conclusão)

  ⎿  # POST /videos/:id/upload/complete — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     `POST /videos/:id/upload/complete` conclui o multipart upload no storage (`ListParts` → `CompleteMultipartUpload` com os ETags em ordem → `HeadObject` para validar o tamanho real contra o declarado), move o vídeo de `draft` para `processing` e enfileira o job `process-video` (`jobId = videoId`) na fila `video-processing` — o gatilho único entre upload e worker. É idempotente: com o vídeo já em `processing`/`ready` devolve `200` com o estado atual sem efeitos colaterais; em `failed` retorna `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`. Partes faltando retornam `409 UPLOAD_INCOMPLETE` mantendo o vídeo em `draft` para retomada. Falha de enqueue (Redis indisponível) não propaga — o sweep re-enfileira vídeos `processing` travados.
     
     ## Test Scenarios
     
     ### 1. Concluir o upload e disparar o processamento
     
     **Setup:** `beforeAll` — `Test.createTestingModule({ imports: [AppModule] }).compile()` + `createNestApplication()` reproduzindo a config global de `main.ts` (`ValidationPipe({ whitelist, forbidNonWhitelisted, transform })`, `DomainExceptionFilter`, `ValidationExceptionFilter`), contra Postgres, MinIO e Redis reais (`QUEUE_PREFIX=streamtube-test` e `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT=http://minio:9000` via `src/test/setup-test-env.ts`; a API só produz — nenhum consumer roda no grafo da API, então o job permanece na fila); a fila é obtida por `app.get(getQueueToken('video-processing'))`; `beforeEach` — `cleanAllTables(dataSource)` + `queue.obliterate({ force: true })` + dois usuários autenticados (dono e outro), cada um com canal, via `createAuthenticatedUser(app)` de `test/helpers/auth.helper.ts` (extraído em SI-03.8); o vídeo do dono é criado via `POST /videos` com `{ "file_name": "clip.mp4", "mime_type": "video/mp4", "size": 1048576 }` (`part_count: 1`) e a parte 1 (1048576 bytes) é enviada por `fetch` `PUT` na URL de `POST /videos/:id/upload/part-urls`; `afterAll` — `queue.obliterate({ force: true })`, `StorageService.deletePrefix( … (+3694 chars)

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Normal completion:** returns `200` with `status: "processing"`, and `GET /videos/:id` then shows `processing`.
- **Queued job:** a `process-video` job exists with its id equal to the video's id.
- **Calling it again:** returns `200` with the current status and doesn't create a second job.
- **Missing part:** returns `409 UPLOAD_INCOMPLETE`; the video stays a draft and `GET /videos/:id/upload` still shows the parts, so the upload can resume.
- **Failed video:** returns `409 INVALID_VIDEO_STATUS`.
- **Redis down when queueing:** still returns `200`, and the video stays in `processing`. The cleanup job (SI-03.17) re-queues it later.
- **Non-owner:** gets `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Real 65 MiB upload:** the integration test uploads 64 MiB + 1 MiB through the presigned URLs and confirms the row, the assembled file and the queued job.

**Things to know:**
- **Every e2e test now loads the queue library:** the app now loads the queue library everywhere, so this is the first real use of the e2e Jest workaround for ES-module packages added in SI-03.5. It works in all 8 suites.
- **Typo in the test plan:** scenario 2.2 uses `failure_reason = 'abandoned_upload'`. The data model value is `upload_abandoned`, so the test uses that.
- **Possible 500 (not fixed):** a client could upload parts smaller than 5 MiB (other than the last) whose sizes still add up to the declared size. Storage then rejects the assembly with `EntityTooSmall`, which comes back as a 500. With the fixed 64 MiB parts only a misbehaving client hits this; mapping it to `UPLOAD_INCOMPLETE` is logged as a separate task.

  ⎿  # GET /videos/:id/stream, /download, /thumbnail — Test Plan
     
     ## Application Overview
     
     Os três endpoints de entrega redirecionam (`302`) o dono para URLs pré-assinadas `GetObject` de curta duração (cliente público, TTL `S3_DELIVERY_URL_TTL_SECONDS`, default 14400), sem consumir banda da API: `stream` aponta para `videos/{id}/original` e o storage atende `Range`/`206` nativamente (reprodução sem download completo); `download` aponta para o mesmo objeto com `response-content-disposition: attachment` contendo o `original_filename`; `thumbnail` aponta para `videos/{id}/thumbnail.jpg`. Os redirects levam `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. A checagem de dono vem antes da de status: não dono sempre recebe `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`; o dono com vídeo fora de `ready` (ou sem thumbnail) recebe `409 VIDEO_NOT_READY`.

● SI-03.13 is done. The owner can now stream, download and get the thumbnail of a `ready` video through `302` redirects to short-lived presigned URLs, so the video bytes never pass through the API. **Created:**
- `src/videos/video-delivery.service.ts`
- `src/videos/content-disposition.util.ts`
- `src/videos/decorators/api-delivery-redirect.decorator.ts`
- tests: `content-disposition.util.spec.ts`, `video-delivery.service.spec.ts`, `video-delivery.service.integration-spec.ts`, `test/videos-delivery.e2e-spec.ts`

**Updated:** `src/videos/videos.controller.ts` (`GET :id/stream`, `:id/download`, `:id/thumbnail`) and `src/videos/videos.module.ts`.

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Redirect:** each route answers `302` with a `Location` starting with `S3_PUBLIC_ENDPOINT`, `X-Amz-Expires=14400` by default, and `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
- **Streaming:** the stream URL with `Range: bytes=0-1023` returns `206`, `Content-Range: bytes 0-1023/4096`, and exactly 1024 bytes.
- **Download:** the download URL responds with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and `filename="clip.mp4"`.
- **Thumbnail:** the thumbnail URL responds `200` with `image/jpeg`.
- **Not ready:** a video in `draft`, `processing` or `failed` returns `409 VIDEO_NOT_READY` on all three routes (9 checks), with no `Location`.
- **Non-owner:** always gets `404 VIDEO_NOT_FOUND`, whether the video is ready or not.
- **Custom link lifetime:** with `S3_DELIVERY_URL_TTL_SECONDS=7200`, all three redirects carry `X-Amz-Expires=7200`.

**Choices worth reviewing:**
- **Shared decorator:** the three routes share their redirect, cache header and OpenAPI annotations through one decorator in `decorators/`, instead of repeating that block three times.
- **Download file name:** the plain `filename` replaces accents, emoji (as a single `_`), quotes, backslashes and control characters with `_`. The exact name goes, percent-encoded, in `filename*`, and line breaks can never reach the header.

● SI-03.16 is done: the `video-worker` starts with `docker compose up -d` and processes a real video end to end on the dev stack. The new tests pass (20), and so do the full suites: 388 unit/integration and 88 e2e. `npm run build` produces `dist/worker.js`, and lint is clean.

**Created:**
- `src/video-processing/video-processing.consumer.ts`
- `src/video-processing/video-processing-worker.module.ts`
- `src/worker.module.ts`
- `src/worker.ts`
- tests: `video-processing.consumer.spec.ts`, `video-processing.consumer.integration-spec.ts`, `src/worker.module.integration-spec.ts`

**Updated:**
- `compose.yaml`: new `video-worker` service, no ports.
- `package.json`: `start:worker` and `start:worker:dev` scripts.

**`Channel` → `User` (as you asked):** `WorkerModule` imports `UsersModule`, the module that owns `User`, the same way the API gets it through `AuthModule`. The consumer integration test compiles the real `WorkerModule` with nothing added on the test side. The integration tests from SI-03.8 to SI-03.15 still register `User` themselves, because they compile those modules on their own; switching them over is optional.

**Dev-stack run:** I started the API only for this check and stopped it afterwards. A small client script on the host did register, confirm e-mail through Mailpit, login, create the video, upload straight to MinIO on `localhost:9000`, then complete.
- **Valid MP4 (12 s, 720p, H.264/AAC):** became `ready` in about 1 s with duration, size, metadata and thumbnail. The stream redirect served a `206` byte range, and the thumbnail came back as `image/jpeg`.
- **MPEG-4 Part 2:** ended `failed` with `unsupported_format` and no thumbnail.
- **Worker stopped:** a completed upload stayed `processing` and its job waited in Redis, so the API never consumes jobs. After restarting the worker, the video became `ready`.

**Shutdown bug found and fixed:** `docker compose stop video-worker` wasn't a clean stop at first.
- **Cause:** `npm` passed the stop signal to `sh -c`, which exited without forwarding it. The worker was then force-killed: no cleanup ran, Redis connections dropped abruptly, and the container exited with code 1.
- **Fix:** the script now runs `exec node --watch …`, so the stop signal reaches the worker. The Compose `command` is unchanged from the plan.
- **Verified:** the worker logs a new `Video worker shut down (SIGTERM)` line, exits with code 0, and its 3 Redis connections close.

**Coverage gap:** the "temporary error retried 5 times, then `processing_error`" criterion is covered by unit tests plus BullMQ's own retry settings. I didn't simulate a real storage outage, since the backoff schedule takes over a minute.

● SI-03.17 is done. The worker now runs a cleanup job every 15 minutes:
- **Abandoned drafts:** drafts left over 24 h are marked `failed` (`upload_abandoned`) and their open upload in storage is closed.
- **Stuck videos:** videos stuck in `processing` for over 1 h are put back on the queue.

All 19 tests in the 5 suites I ran pass, `tsc` and lint are clean, and nothing was left behind in Redis or MinIO.

**Created** (in `src/video-processing/reconciliation/`):
- `reconciliation.constants.ts`
- `video-reconciliation.service.ts`, the cleanup logic
- `video-reconciliation.consumer.ts`, which runs it from the queue
- `video-reconciliation.scheduler.ts`, which registers the 15-minute schedule
- the 3 test files

**Updated:**
- `src/video-processing/video-processing-worker.module.ts`: the new queue and providers, in the worker only, so the API is unchanged.
- The two SI-03.16 worker test files: they now also clear the new queue, because the schedule is registered every time the worker starts.

**What the tests confirm:**
- **Drafts:** a 25 h draft is marked `failed`, its row is kept and its upload no longer exists in storage. A 23 h draft is untouched, and a draft whose upload was already closed is still marked `failed`.
- **Race with completion:** if an upload completes while the cleanup is running, the video stays `processing` and isn't overwritten.
- **Stuck videos:** a video processing for 2 h gets a new job with the video's id. A second run doesn't duplicate it, and one processing for 30 min is left alone.
- **Errors:** an error closing one upload doesn't stop the others; that draft is retried on the next run.

**On the dev stack:** after two `docker compose restart video-worker`, Redis holds exactly one `reconcile-videos` schedule every 900000 ms, and each restart shut down cleanly.

**Things to know:**
- **First run:** the queue library lines its 15-minute runs up with the clock, so the first cleanup happens up to 15 minutes after startup (about 10 minutes later in this run), not immediately on boot.
- **Queue down during a run:** if putting a stuck video back on the queue fails, the whole cleanup run fails and the next one, 15 minutes later, retries.
- **Leftover files removed:** I deleted 3 sets of files from MinIO left by my SI-03.16 check. Their database rows had already been wiped by test cleanup, because the tests share the dev database.
